2053. RYLEY COOPER was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, 1 kettle, value 10 s., the goods of Thomas Pain.
THOMAS PAIN . I keep the Lady Grey beer shop at Crayford-heath. I had this kettle safe on the 7th of August ( looking at it)—I saw the Prisoner about my premises that day—the kettle was brought back to me the next morning.
WILLIAM JAMES ( police-constable R 83.) On Wednesday morning the 8th of August, I found the prisoner in a shed with this kettle tied round his body, in this handkerchief as it is now—he said he got it from his aunt in Nottingham-lane.
Prisoner. Yes; the boy that I had it of told me to say so.
(The prisoner received a good character.)
GUILTY . Aged 11.— Confined One Month.

2054. JOHN BUNTON was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, 1 basket, value 1 s. 6 d.; and 1 bushel of peas, value 2 s. 6 d.; the goods of Benjamin James.
BENJAMIN JAMES . My peas were put on the cart, and when they came to market it was deficient—they were mine, and the basket was mine.
JOHN GREEN . I live at Plumstead. Between eleven and twelve o'clock at night on the 25th of July, I was driving the prosecutor's cart with fruit and peas—I heard a noise when I got towards the Ship and Billet, on the Woolwich-road—I jumped down, and the peas were gone—I looked about, and saw them in the ditch under the hedge, and the prisoner was there-no one else was there—I asked him what he had done with the peas—he said he had seen none—I pulled him down as he was getting over a stile—I had seen him go from the cart as soon as I jumped down, and he went towards the ditch from the tail of the cart, just by where I saw the peas-and I saw him in the ditch when I went to him—I caught hold of him, and called the ostler.
GEORGE M'MILLAN ( police-constable K 201.) I was called, and went to the ditch—I found the basket of peas, which the prosecutor owned.
Prisoner. You asked what I had got in my pocket, and I said a rope, and you wanted to see it. Witness. There was a pair of stockings found just at the back of the cart where he took the peas from, and he said he had lost a pair-next morning they were shown to him, and he said they were his.
GUILTY . Aged 50.— Confined Three Months.

2055. ANN SHERRATT was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of June, 1 basket, value 1 s.; one collar, value 2 s.; 1 gown, value 5 s.; 1 cap, value 6 d.; 1 pair of stockings, value 6 d.; 2 neckerchiefs, value 6 d.; 3 handkerchiefs, value 1 s.; 2 printed books, value 1 s.; and 1 caul, value 2 s.; the goods of Thomas Redgrove.
SARAH LYDIA REDGROVE . I am the wife of Thomas Redgrove, a pensioner at Greenwich. I went to see my husband, and slept in a room rented by Margaret Sidebottom, in East-street—the prisoner came and knocked at the door of the room on the morning of the 18th of June, and called Mrs. Sidebottom—the boy got up to see who it was, and said she could not come in—I said, "Let her in"—I got up—she said she came from Chatham, and was so tired she could not go further—I let her in from
compassion—I then put the articles stated, which were my Sunday things, into my basket I left them while I went to tell my husband I was going to down-when I came back the things were gone, also the basket and the prisoner—they are not here—I have heard nothing of them—I was away ten minutes or a quarter of an hour—I went and came back immediately—it was about half-past eight o'clock when I went out of my room.
ELIZABETH HODGES . I live near Mrs. Sidebottom. I remember on the morning of the 18th of June, about a quarter-past eight o'clock, seeing the prisoner come from Mrs. Sidebottom's house—she had a basket under her arm, tied up with a piece of cord—it appeared to be full and heavy.
SARAH LYDIA REDOROVE re-examined. It was a market-basket—I tied it up in a white handkerchief—there was a cord in the back-room, I did not notice whether that was gone when I came back again.
Prisoner. I had lodged with this woman five weeks—that morning I called; but as for seeing any thing about these things I am innocent.
GUILTY .* Aged 50.— Confined Six Months.

2057. GEORGE MANNING was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of July, 13lbs. weight of glass, value 1 s., the goods of Nathaniel Wegg.
JOHN HYDE . I am servant to Nathaniel Wegg, and live at Greenwich. On the 24th of July I had a quantity of broken glass in a tub—I lost thirteen or fourteen pounds of it—here is some that I can swear to-here is one piece with a string tied round it by myself, and another with a knob.
GRACE REEVES . I heard a noise of some one getting over Mr. Wegg's fence—I saw the prisoner in the yard—he spoke to some one on the other side of the fence—he had a bundle with him, which sounded to me like glass—I gave information.
AGNES FENTON . I keep a marine store-shop, in Hog-lane, Greenwich. The prisoner brought this broken glass to me, and I bought it, it was between nine and ten o'clock on the 24th of July—I gave him 6 3/4 d. for it.
Prisoner's Defence. A man asked me if I would earn a shilling, to help him to pull down two coppers, which I did—he then took me into the yard, and got a half-bushel basket, he filled it with iron, and we went to Mrs. Fenton's and sold the iron for 7 1/2 d.—we then went and pulled down another copper, and he gave me the glass to go and sell.
GUILTY . Aged 23.— Confined Three Months.

ELIZABETH BROWN . I saw the milk-jug taken from the prosecutor's by either Margaret M'Cormick or Lee, I cannot tell which, it was passed from one to the other so quickly—they were playing about the street at the time—they were in a state of great destitution.
GEORGE PEDDER ( police-constable R 108.) I went up the road, and saw Clark take the jug from under her petticoats.

2061. JANE PEAD and JANE JONES were indicted for stealing on the 20th of August, 1 tobacco-box, value 4 d.; 4 shillings, and 7 six-pences; the goods and monies of James Langford, from his person.
JAMES LANGFORD . I sell vegetables in the street. On Monday afternoon, the 20th of August, I met the two prisoners at the bottom of Flagonrow, Deptford—I suppose it was past two o'clock—they asked me to give them a drop of beer—I knew their faces, and I knew they lived in the neighbourhood—I took them into the beer-shop, and gave them a pot of beer—I had a tobacco-box, four shillings, and seven sixpences—we went down Grove-lane, to the King's Head, and had another pot there—I laid my head down and went to sleep—they were both on my left-hand side—I cannot say who was nearest to me—I am sure when I put my hand down that my tobacco-box was safe, in my left-hand trowser's pocket—I did not
awake till it was getting quite late, getting on for six o'clock—my box was then gone.
MICHAEL RENNESON . I am a labourer. I saw the prisoners at the King's Head, where the man fell asleep—I was in the room before they came into it—he put his head down and fell asleep—the women were on the left side of him—I saw Pead put her hand into his pocket, and take out his tobacco-box—the other was sitting by the side of her at the time—I saw her open the box and count the money on the table-four shillings and seven sixpences—they then took the box and went out together—I did not wake the man, because Pead said, when she counted the money, that she considered that he ought to have more, and I thought she was his wife.
Pead. Q. What did he say to you when you said he was robbed; did he not tell you it was all right? A. Yes he did—I told him directly after they were gone—he did not miss the box before I told him, and then he said it was all right—I awoke him directly after the women went out, about half-past five o'clock.
WILLIAM MURRAY . I am a policeman. I took Jones into custody on Tuesday morning last at the Oxford Arms-Pead was taken by Marshall on Tuesday evening—I found 2 d. and a duplicate on Jones, for two pairs of pattens.
Pead. I am unfortunate, it is true, but I am not guilty of this—I had been at the poor-house at Deptford, and meant to go to the Refuge.
PEAD— GUILTY . Aged 18.
JONES— GUILTY . Aged 21.
Confined Six Months.
Before Mr. Recorder.

2063. THOMAS ENDERWICK was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 14 yards of woollen cloth, value 13 l. the goods of William Henry Whiteman, his master.
WILLIAM HENRY WHITEMAN . I am a draper and tailor, and live at Woolwich. The prisoner was my apprentice—on the 20th of August, in the evening, when I returned from London I found the prisoner in custody—I afterwards saw these three pieces of cloth—I heard that it was gone and then I missed it—this is it—it is my property.
MARY WHITEMAN . I am the prosecutor's wife. On the 20th of August, I was in the prisoner's bed-room between three and four o'clock in the afternoon—the prisoner came down from the workshop into that room, which was very unusual—he saw me and he quitted the room very shortly after—I waited till he was gone, and looked into his box—it was without a key—I found in it a piece of cloth tied in a handkerchief—I took it into the cutting-room to one of our young men, and gave it to him—he went down and found in the prisoner's box another piece of cloth.
Cross-examined by MR. DOANE. Q. How many young men had you? A. About a dozen—I found this piece of cloth in the box just after he left the room—I did not see him do any thing to the box, but I saw him move another box.
COURT. Q. He moved a box which he had no business to do? A. Yes-no one occupied that room as a bed-room but the prisoner.
JOSEPH M'DIVETT . I am in the employ of Mr. Whiteman. In consequence of something said so me by Mrs. Whiteman, I went into the prisoner's room, looked into his box, and found a bundle of cloth tied up in a silk handkerchief—this is the handkerchief—I believe it is the prisoner's—this is the cloth—the box had no lock on it—that room was only used for the prisoner to sleep in, but my box was in that room-when I went with the prisoner into his bed-room, he said he thought he should be as well out of the country as in it, and then he said, "Pray forgive me this time; tell the policeman to go away."
WILLIAM THOMAS CHITTENDEN . I am a constable of Woolwich. I produce the cloth—I found a third piece in the prisoner's box—I searched him and found some keys which opened two small boxes of his-as we were going to the watch-house, he said they could not prove that he stole the cloth, and he might be as well out of the country as in it—I asked him, when I took the keys, what they belonged to, and he said to the two boxes belonging to him—I opened one of them, and in that I found this third piece of cloth.
WILLIAM HENRY WHITEMAN . These are all mine—they all match where they have been haggled off with a knife—I had seen them safe on Monday night, and they were all right then—the prisoner had been with me eighteen months.
MARY WHITEMAN re-examined. Nothing was kept in the room where the prisoner slept beside his boxes and the foreman's—it was used only as a bed-room—the prisoner had three boxes there—I went to the room to put a clean curtain up to the window—I did not say anything to the prisoner.
GUILTY . Aged 18.— Transported for Seven Years.
SURREY CASES.
Before Mr. Recorder.

[trialAccount t18380820-2067]
2067. JOHN WILSON was indicted for burglariously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of William Henry Cable, about the hour of eleven in the night of the 11th of August, at St. Olave, Southwark, with intent to steal, and stealing therein, 27lbs. weight of lead pipe, value 6 s., his goods.
WILLIAM HENRY CABLE . I live at No. 3, Cross-keys-passage, Bermondsey-street. I keep the house—it is in the parish of St. Olave, South-wark—on the 11th of August I left home, at a quarter to nine or nine o'clock at night—I locked the street-door, and took the key with me—my brother, James Edward Cable, lodges in the house with me—we are the only two who occupy it—he had been out all day—it was not quite nine o'clock when I left—I generally leave the key at the Cross-keys-passage when I am out, but I took it with me on this occasion—I returned just upon eleven o'clock—I found the house open, and my brother James inside, all in a gore of blood, and a policeman inside with him—I missed fifteen feet of lead pipe, weighing twenty-seven or twenty-eight pounds, from inside the house-nobody could get it without effecting an entrance into the house—it was safe when I went out—it has not been found—it was carried away.
JAMES EDWARD CABLE . I am brother of last witness. On Saturday evening, the 11th of August, I returned home, between ten and eleven o'clock—I found the door open, and two persons walked out—the prisoner was the third person who came out of the house, and I collared him—he immediately struck me in the mouth with his fist—I kept hold of him, and a struggle took place between us—I found myself all over blood, and my head was cut—that was not done with a blow from the fist—we struggled for a quarter of a hour—he got me down, and got upon me, and called " Bill " who I supposed was one of his companions—I became insensible for a few minutes, and cannot tell when I got the blow which produced the blood—the struggle was outside, in the street—I did not let go of him till he was apprehended.
Prisoner. I contradict it—he struck me first in the head, I threw him, and I suppose that was the way he got cut in the head—he then laid hold of me and shook me.
AUGUSTUS SAXBY ( police-constable M 49.) On the 11th of August I went into Bermondsey-street, and found the prisoner there-two of our constables had hold of him by the collar, when I came up opposite the court where the prosecutor lives—I searched the spot where the scuffle took place, and found a skeleton key, which I produce—it opened the street door very easily—I tried it—I saw James Cable—he was all in a gore of blood—the back of his coat was smothered with blood, and his face was bloody.
ANN MARIA CABLE . I am the prosecutor's mother. I was with my son James on the Saturday evening, and saw the three persons come out of the house—the prisoner was the last—my son followed him, and he struck him directly—I assisted my son, and then he struck me, and bent my bonnet-l left my son and went to look for a policeman, and when I came back the prisoner was gone with the policeman, and my son was all in a gore of blood—there was a cut in the top of his head—I cannot say how it was done—I think it must have been done against the edge of the bricks when he fell—it was quite dark in-doors at the time.
Prisoner. I was not in the house at all. Witness. I saw him come out my son followed him as he came out.
Prisoner. I took nothing—there were two men in the house with an aprons on—they called me, and asked me to fetch a penny box of lucifers-all at once they ran out and left me there—this man took hold of me and hit me, and I hit him again, and the mother hit me, and I threw him, as I had both upon me. Witness. I could not see whether the other two had aprons on—they did not appear trades people—I found no box of lucifers the prisoner came out of the door of the house—he had one foot over the sill when my son caught him.
(John Upton, steam-engine and boiler manufacturer, Sun Wharf, Batter-sea, gave the prisoner a good character.)
GUILTY . Aged 17.— Recommended to mercy by the Jury.— Transported for Ten Years.
[trialAccount t18380820-2068]
2068. NORAH CALLAN was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of August, at Lambeth, 1 cloak, value 6 s.; 1 cap, value 1 s.; and 6 sovereigns; the goods and monies of James Devine, her master, in his dwelling-house.
JAMES DEVINE . I am a pig-dealer, and live in Park-street, Kennington-cross, in the parish of Lambeth. I keep the house—the prisoner was in my service for a fortnight—on the 3rd of August I went out with my wife, between seven and eight o'clock, leaving the prisoner at home—we returned between eleven and twelve o'clock, and she was gone—she had locked the room door, and taken the key with her—I broke the door open, and found my box broken open, and six sovereigns gone—my wife missed a cloak and cap, which have been found.
MARY DEVINE . I am the prosecutor's wife. I came home with him on the 3rd of August, about twelve o'clock, and missed a cloak and cap—I found the prisoner the same morning on board the Cork steamer, at St. Katherine's Dock, with the cloak on her shoulder—the money had been in a gallipot, in a box, in my bed-room, which was broken open—the cloak was in another box—she had the cap in her bundle—these are them—( looking at them.)
THOMAS FITZGERALD ( police-constable L 166.) In consequence of information I went to the Cork steamer, in St. Katherine's Docks, on the 4th of August, and there found the prisoner—I found the cloak on her, and in a purse round her neck I found four sovereigns, twelve half-crowns, two shillings and fourpence—the cap was in her bundle, and the key of the door.
Prisoner's Defence. Another one put me up to it—I do not know whether the things belong to her more than a child.
GUILTY . Aged 27.— Recommended to mercy by the Prosecutor.
Transported for Ten Years.
[trialAccount t18380820-2069]
2069. JOHN BARNARD was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of July, 1 handkerchief, value 6 s., the goods of William Rennell, from his person; and that he had been previously convicted of felony.
THOMAS HESSELTINE . I am a tin-plate worker, and live in Ann-street, St. George's-in—the-East. I was in Blackman-street, on the 13th of July, at a quarter to ten o'clock in the evening, and saw the prisoner in company with another, by St. George's Church, attempting a gentleman's pocket—I followed them down Blackman-street, and saw the prisoner take a handKerchief
out of the prosecutor's pocket—I told a policeman of it, who was on the opposite side of the way—I do not know what he did with the handkerchief—it was red and white one.
HENRY HUNT ( police-constable M 91.) In consequence of information from Hesseltine, I watched the prisoner, and saw him in the act of taking handkerchief from Mr. Rennell's pocket—I made towards him, and saw him take it—he looked round, saw me, and went up Suffolk-street—I ran after him—he was close to the other man, and could have given him the handkerchief—they appeared acting together, both keeping close on the gentleman—I secured him.
WILLIAM RENNELL . I live in Windmill-place, Camberwell. I was in Blackman-street on the 13th of July—the policeman spoke to me, and I missed my handkerchief—on turning round I saw the prisoner—he ran off as fast as he could.
Prisoner. I was merely going to get my brother's washing—I saw the mob, and went up, and the policeman said, "This is the one, I think."
HENRY HUNT re-examined. He is the man—there was a mob when he ran away—I never lost sight of him.
PETER LOGAN ( police-sergeant M 12.) I produce a certificate of the prisoner's former conviction—I was present when he was tried and convicted last June Sessions—( read)—he is the person.
GUILTY . Aged 18.— Transported for Ten Years.
[trialAccount t18380820-2070]
2070. JOHN FREDERICK BOX was indicted for feloniously forging and uttering a certain acquittance and receipt, for the sum of 3 l. 14 s., with intent to defraud John Giles Pilcher, and another.
WILLIAM THACKER . I live in Willow-walk, Orange-hill, Bermondsey. if he prisoner was my apprentice—I made out a bill to Messrs. Pilcher, of the organ's-lane, for one dozen of half-firkin casks, at 1 s. each, and receipted the bill—that bill has been lost—( looking at a bill)—this is a copy of the one I wrote, but no part of it whatever is my hand-writing—I believe it to be the prisoner's hand-writing—I have seen him write, and from my knowledge of his writing I believe it to be his.
Cross-examined by MR. CHAMBERS. Q. When did you see him write? A. When he signed his indentures—I have compared it with his indentures, that leads me to conclude it is his—I am a cooper by business.
COURT. Q. Have you kept your memory alive by looking at what you have seen him write? A. Yes—I am not able to speak to it on oath, but I believe it is his-if I had seen it elsewhere I should have taken it to be his writing—I did not authorise that bill to be drawn out-Messrs. Pilcher were indebted to me for a dozen of half-firkins—the prisoner did not pay me for them—I did not give him authority to sign my name for money received on my account.
MR. CHAMBERS. Q. I think your son manages your business? A. Yes, he is here—the prisoner received money in the business, and also the other apprentice, in my absences—I cannot say whose hand-writing the first line of this bill is—it is not mine—I believe it is not my son's-nor the second, third, or fourth line-with the exception of the paleness of the ink I do not think there is any difference in the writing—I have got my son's hand-writing in my pocket, with the signature of this indenture—I have not been comparing that with the bill, only before the
Magistrate, but the Magistrate would not go into that—I did not do that because I suspected it was my son's hand-writing—the prisoner confessed to me that he drew the money and forged the bill, and said my son persuaded him to do it—he told me that in New Walk, Dock-head—I took my son's hand-writing to show that it was not his writing—the prisoner told me he had destroyed my bill that I made out for twelve shilings—the Magistrate said he would have no comparison of hand-writing—I had no hand-writing to compare but the indenture—it was before I went to the Magistrate that the prisoner told me he had written it—I promised to forgive him if he told the truth—I cannot tell how it became a question whether it was my son's hand-writing—the prisoner told me he wrote it, but I could not swear to the hand-writing—their hand-writing is very much alike—my son has had a quarrel with the prisoner, and just after the Coronation he took him before the Magistrate for an assault, because he would not do his work—he had been apprenticed since December—I had 5 l. premium, 4 l. from Newington-school, and 1 l. from his mother—I was to receive 5 l. more after twelve months—he was to learn the art of a cooper—I was brought up to the trade and served seven years—I have a situation in the employ of Thomas Jones and Co., oil merchants—they require a cooper—my private business is conducted by my son—I have been looking at my son's hand-writing since I went before the Magistrate—my son pays me all the money he receives—it is only a trifling business.
JOHN GILES PILCHER . I received this bill from the prisoner and paid him 3 l. 14 s. taking off 4 s. discount—it was precisely in this state when produced to me—it is signed by Russell, who is in our concern, and if it had been 100 l. I should have paid it—it is his duty to examine and mine to pay.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you know the prisoner before? A. I never saw him before to my knowledge—I am quite sure it was him—it was about half-past twelve o'clock.
JAMES RUSSELL . I am warehouseman to Messrs. Pilcher, of Morgan-lane. On Saturday, the 11th of August, the prisoner brought this bill to my desk—the only charge in it then, was in one line for 2 1/2 dozen of firkins, 1 l. 4 s.—I put my name in the corner for him to go and receive twentyfour shillings-two items have been added since, making it 3 l. 18 s.—I was at home when some firkins came—the prisoner brought part of them, which made up the dozen—I now find a hand-writing on the bill for 3 l. 18 s. instead of 1 l. 4 s.
Cross-examined. Q. Can you recollect who brought the rest of the casks, was it the prosecutor's son? A. No—I do not think he has been on our premises for some months—this was a little business we gave him—we thought we were dealing with Thacker, the younger—he formerly used to work on our premises.
WILLIAM THACKER, JUN . I carry on the coopering business on my father's account-no part of the receipt produced is my hand-writing, nor was any of it written by my direction, or with my knowledge—the prisoner never paid me this 3 l. 14 s.—he never produced the bill to me—I first saw it in Mr. Russell's hands—the hand-writing does not bear the least resemblance to mine—I have no knowledge of the prisoner's writing—I never saw him write—I have had several quarrels with the prisoner.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you recollect witnessing an indenture of apprenticeship?
A. Yes, I signed my name to it—I have not been studying the hand-writing on that indenture—I cannot say that my father has shown it to me—he told me he had brought it here—he did not exactly tell me why he brought it—he did not tell me it was to compare with the prisoner's hand-writing—my father produced the indenture at Union Hall—the prisoner stated before the Magistrate that I told him to do this—there was no dispute about my hand-writing—I did not tell him to write it—I have had a good many quarrels with him—I have received a great many bills during the time I have served my father—I made these half-firkins, but did not carry them home—I have received a great many bills from the Messrs. Pilcher—I discovered this last Monday week—I do not receive the money—my mother receives it—either I or the apprentice go for it—Messrs. Pilcher did not understand that the transaction was with me, they knew to the contrary—Mr. Russell is their managing man—he knew it was on my father's account—I received the order from the ware-houseman—my father told me on the Tuesday night that the prisoner said I directed him to do this—it was not till Thursday morning that we went before the Magistrate—I was sworn before the Magistrate—I cannot say whether my deposition was taken in writing.
Q. Did you receive 11 s. 6 d. from the prisoner? A. No—my mother told me he had given her 11 s. 6 d.—I never saw the bill before the addition was made to it—I did not know of these words being added—I did not give the prisoner pen and ink to put any words in—I met him in Page's-walk after he had got the money-about twenty yards from our house, and asked him if he had received the money my father had made out the bill for—he said, "Yes"—I did not say "Give me and my mother 11 s. 6 d. each, and do what you please with the rest"—my mother had made out the bill for 12 s., and he said they had taken off 6 d. discount.
JAMES RUSSELL re-examined. When I affixed my name to the bill the prisoner went away from me—I was very busy, and did not look which way he went—I gave him the bill to go across to the counting-house for the money—there was nothing to prevent his going away, and making the addition to it.
( The bill being read, was for two dozen York firkins, 2 l.; two dozen do., 1 l.; 4 s.; one dozen tar, 14 s.; total, 3 l. 18 s.; discount, 4 s. 0 d.; balance 3 l. 14 s.)
GUILTY of uttering. Aged 16.— Transported for Seven Years.
Before Mr. Justice Bosanquet.
[trialAccount t18380820-2071]
2071. WILLIAM CASEY was indicted for forging a receipt for 2 l., with intent to defraud William Boers.-2nd COUNT, calling it a certain accountable receipt for money.
WIILIAM BOERS . I belong to the Britannia steam-vessel, which on the 28th of June laid at Horsleydown. I saw the prisoner on board the vessel off Davis's Wharf, on the Surrey side of the river—he said he had got a letter from Mr. Walters, of Rotterdam, to buy some table-cloths for him, that he had sent him 5 l. 10 s., and if he was short of money he must come to me—I asked him how much he was short—he said, 2 l.—I gave him 2 l.—he said he would bring me a parcel of table-cloths—he asked for some paper, which I gave him, and he wrote a letter to Mr. Walters, and gave
me a receipt for 2 l., for Mr. Walters—I have the letter and receipt here—( looking at them)—he never brought me the parcel of table-cloths—I saw him again on Saturday morning, about eight o'clock—he asked me if I was not sorry about the 2 l. that he had not brought the parcel—I was going back to Rotterdam, and expected to take the parcel to Mr. Walters—I went on shore with him, and we drank two bottles of ginger-beer—he then asked me if I had got time to fetch the parcel with him—I said, "No, I must be on board again"—he did not tell me where the parcel was to be fetched from—I went on board, leaving him on shore—I saw him again about one o'clock that day—he said he had got a parcel for me—he went into his boat, and I did not see him again—he had no parcel with him—he is a traveller, and comes over with horses and families—he often comes by my steam-boat—I have known him about five or six years—I took the letter and receipt with me to Rotterdam, and showed them both to Mr. Walters—I did not get any money from him—he is now at Rotterdam—I saw no more of the prisoner till he was in custody at the Thames Police-office.
Prisoner. Q. How many times did I come on board on the Saturday? A. Three times-you brought nothing with you the last time—I did not see you take any parcel to the head steward—I do not recollect your saying in the morning that there would be another parcel to go to Rotterdam—the mate did not tell me you had brought a parcel to the head steward to take to Rotterdam—I told the captain about this—( letter read)-"To Mr. Walters. London, 30th June. Sir, This morning a parcel came from the Merchant's Hall. I paid 7 l. 10 s.; the balance of which Mr. Boers paid to me for you. Yours, truly, WILLIAM SMITH." -Receipt-"Received of William Boers, 2 l., for Mr. Walters. 28th June. W. SMITH."
GEORGE WALKER JUDGE . I am a policeman. I received these two papers from Boers—I apprehended the prisoner on another case—I did not tell him what it was for.
Prisoner's Defence. I was to go to the prosecutor and give him the parcel—I went and informed him of it, and asked whether he would take it—he said he would, and I took it on board—I told him before I took it there was a balance owing, and he paid me 2 l., the balance of the amount.
NOT GUILTY .

[trialAccount t18380820-2074]
2074. WILLIAM MILLER and CHARLES ROSS were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of July, 1 cask, value 3 s.; and 22 gallons of oil value 3 l. 5 s.; the goods of Sir Charles Price and others, their masters.-2nd COUNT, stating them to be the goods of John Scovell and others.
MR. PAYNE conducted the Prosecution.
CHAPLIN WOOD . I am in the employ of Messrs. Scovell, of Seymour's Wharf, Tooley-street—I have the charge of the gate. On Saturday morning, the 14th of July, I was near the gate, and saw the prisoner Miller roll a cask of oil outside the gate—he and Ross put it into a truck-Ross was outside with the truck—they went up the lane—I followed them across Tooley-street, and informed the police—I had seen them working there the day before—they were in the employ of Sir Charles Price.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Had there been any oil spilled on the ground? A. Not that I am aware of—I did not look.
Cross-examined by MR. CHAMBERS. Q. Did they continue together? A. Yes, to the corner of Magdalen-street, and put the truck down—they then came back—I do not know where they went to.
HENRY SMITH . I live in Chicksand-street, and work at the wharf. On Saturday morning, the 14th of July, Miller spoke to me in Stoney-lane, and asked if I knew a person who would earn a shilling—I said, "What for?"—he said, "To draw a truck with a cask of oil on it"—I said I would have no objection to do it—he said he would give me a shilling and a glass of gin—I went with him, and found the truck in Tooley-street—he said I was to take it to his house—I went as far as King-street in the Borough—I left Miller in Tooley-street-when I got to King-street, a policeman stopped me-Ross then came up, and desired roe to take it back again, but the constable took me and the truck to the station-house.
ANDREW BOURKE ( police-constable M 154.) I stopped Smith in King-street, with the truck, on the 14th of July-Ross came and told him to take the oil back to Seymour's Wharf—that was before I stopped it—I then stepped forward, and said, "Where to?" and he said, "To Seymour's Wharf"—I said, "It has already come from there, you must go with me"—I had information before—Mr. Rumsey has seen the same cask of oil.
JOHN WOODHOUSE ( police-sergeant M 13.) I was in King-street, on the 14th of July, between eight and nine o'clock in the morning-Miller was coming towards me—I stopped him, and asked if he was not one of the persons engaged in moving the oil—he said yes, he was, and he was going to the station-house to order it to be brought back again, by order of Mr. Partington—he said it was taken away by mistake-as we were going along, he said it was of no consequence, it was mere pickings-up, what was allowed to coopers—I asked him what it was worth—he said about ten-pence a gallon, that there was twenty-two gallons, and it was going to a man at Lambeth to make cart-grease of.
JAMES PARTINGTON . I have the management of Messrs. Scovell's Wharf. I had oil there belonging to Sir Charles Price and Co.—I had no other oil of that description—I know of no privilege that coopers have of picking up any thing-if there is any leakage it is taken up and sold as
other oil—on the morning of the 14th of July, Mr. Hoskins brought Miller to me, and told me he had taken a cask of oil, which he had no right to do-Miller said, "The cask is come back again, for God's sake do not make any thing of it, the cask is come back, it is all right"—I told him to go back to the wharf—I did not tell him to go and get it from the station-house—I understood him the cask was come back.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Was King-street in his way? A. No, it is up in the Borough, from three to five hundred yards off—we landed the oil in Sir Charles Price's name—I do know where it came from.
WILLIAM SAMUEL RUMSEY . I am foreman to Sir Charles Price and Co. He has four partners—they had some oil at Messrs. Smell's Wharf—on the 14th the prisoner had not been employed in fetching it away-Miller was a foreman cooper, and Ross was a cooper—I gave neither of the prisoners authority to fetch oil on the 14th—I saw the cask of oil at the station-house—there were twenty-two gallons—it was exactly the same oil as we have at Seymour's Wharf—it is worth about 3 l. 5 s.—it was pale seal oil, not pickings-up.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Were you on the spot when this oil was moved? A. No-Miller had been in Sir Charles Price's service about four months—there is sometimes pickings-up.
Cross-examined by MR. CHAMBERS. Q. How long had Ross been in your service? A. I believe about twenty-two months—he was one of the coopers, and Miller was foreman of the coopers—there were no other coopers employed at the wharf—the oil had been removing three or four days-Ross was not there the first or second day of landing—I think he went down on Thursday morning—I heard of the loss on that Saturday-about a quarter past ten o'clock Ross come and told Mr. Price of it—I and Mr. Price went to the wharf—I and Ross went to the police-office—he was ordered into custody.
COURT. Q. He came and told Mr. Price of the loss? A. Yes, but Miller had been examined when I and Ross got to the wharf.
MR. CLARKSON to JAMES PARTINGTON. Q. Did you see the oil on the wharf? A. Yes, for several days—I cannot speak to the identity of the cask, so as to be able to say it contained the oil—it was not the cask that was imported—it was a cask taken off the wharf—I never saw the cash that belonged to Sir Charles Price, or the cask in which the oil was.
MR. CHAMBERS. Q. Do you swear there was not as much as twenty gallons leaked? A. No.
MR. PAYNE. Q. You had no other oil except what belonged to Sir. Charles Price? A. No.
WILLIAM SAMUEL RUMSEY re-examined. I saw the oil—it was exactly like that belonging to Sir Charles Price.
MILLER— GUILTY . Aged 31.— Confined Six Months.
ROSS— NOT GUILTY .

2076. JAMES TAYLOR was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of July 2 gowns, value 11 s.; 1 cloak, value 10 s.; and 1 pocket, value 6 d.; the goods of Emily Manser; and that he had been before convicted of felony.
EMILY MANSER . I am servant to Mr. Hobbs, at Kew, Surrey. I had a merino cloak, two gowns, and a pocket—I saw them safe about five o'clock, on the 21st of July, in my box, in a passage even with the kitchen—I missed them on the 24th—these are my things—( looking at them)—I do not know the prisoner.
THOMAS PARSONS . I am a market-gardener. On the 22nd of July I pursued the prisoner from Barnes, and took him at Putney, with a bundle, containing these things—he said it was his property.
Cross-examined by MR. PAYNE. Q. What day of the week was that? A. Sunday, about twelve o'clock-Barnes is three miles from Putney, by the way he went—I was running—I was very little out of breath—I did not lose sight of him—I came up to him at the Half Moon, Putney—I found the bundle in the passage—I did not take it from him—he was standing by it—he said it was his, and I should not have it—he came through Barnes a little after twelve o'clock—I am not a constable.
JOHN FRANKS ( police-constable V 124.) I took the prisoner.
JOHN PASCOE ( police-constable T 19.) I produce the certificate of the prisoner's former conviction, from Mr. Clerk's office—( read)—the prisoner is the man who was tried and convicted.
Cross-examined. Q. When was that? A. On the 22nd September, 1834—I have known him nine years.
GUILTY . Aged 22.— Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18380820-2077]
2077. EDWARD THOMAS was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of July, 1 tarpaulin, value 1 l. 15 s., the goods of Henry Dudin.
MR. BALLANTINE conducted the Prosecution.
HENRY DUDIN . I am a lighterman and wharfinger, at Horsleydown, and reside at Sydenham. On the 20th of July I saw two of my tarpaulins doubled up—I gave orders for their being put into the punt named the Hope, under my own eye—it was lying in the lighter-road, Horsleydown—the punt was shoved off, and remained there till the following Tuesday, when I received some information, and went along-side—I went to where the tarpaulins ought to have been, and the small one was gone—one of them is here, and it is mine—the prisoner is a lighterman—I do not know that he works any where—I have seen him at George Stairs—the premises adjoining these stairs—I never let or lent any one the tarpaulin.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you know a man named Stemp? A. I never saw him till at the police-office, when he was in custody, as I was informed—I saw him at the bar—the prisoner and the wharfinger's man were in custody—I do not recollect that the prisoner said a word—he was not asked in my hearing of what size it was-at the first examination I asked the Magistrate as a particular favour to let it stand over—I was present during the first and second examination—I was not absent during any time.
Q. Was not the prisoner asked whether that was the Mr. Stemp to whom the property was consigned, and did he not say he was? A. I think I can most conscientiously say that he was not asked one single
question, and that he did not say that during the examination—he did not say that was the Mr. Stemp that was present—I saw this tarpaulin last on the 20th of July on my own barge deck—I gave orders to two of my servants to fold it up, and put it on board, as I stood on the bage—we had a great deal of corn landing at the time.
MR. BALLANTINE. Q. How came you to have the prisoner apprehended? A. I did not have him apprehended—the first information I had of his being charged was from some other person—I did not know there was a robbery committed—I do not know Stemp—he was taken as a prisoner—he was locked up a week, and then discharged, as I was informed, for receiving seven quarters of wheat.
JOSEPH DANIEL IVES . I am a Thames-police surveyor. About half-past six o'clock on the morning of the 23rd of July I was on duty in the river—I saw a barge at Bellmont-wharf, Nine Elms—some coal-heaven were carrying something up in sacks—I landed at the railroad, and went on the wharf adjoining Messrs. Frances and White's wharf—I could see into the bottom of the barge—I went into a granary, and found some wheat I had been looking for—I then went into the coal-heaver's lobby, and found this tarpaulin, which the prosecutor has identified—it has his same in full length upon it—it was in the back of the lobby, with some sacks in front of it—you could not see it without going into the lobby—I was at the Bellmont wharf between two and three o'clock that same afternoon, and saw the prisoner come on the wharf—he walked up towards me—I asked him if he was the lighterman that brought up the wheat in the morning—he said he was—that he brought it for a Mr. Stemp, and he was employed by a gentleman he did not know, whom he met at Shad Thames—he asked him if he would take a barge with seven quarters of wheat to Bellmont-wharf, and get it landed for Mr. Stemp, and meet him on the day after at the India-house, and he would settle with him—I asked him what barge he brought it from—he said the Thomas, which was the barge I had seen—he said he did not know to whom she belonged—I asked him whether he had any covering to the wheat—he said "No"—I asked him how he came by Mr. Dudin's tarpaulin—he said he had forgotten that, he did not know it was Mr. Dudin's, but he knew him very well—Mr. Dudin's name was upon it—I asked him whether he could read—he said "Yes," but he did not look outside the barge to see the name—I told him Mr. Dudin's tarpaulin was well marked, and he made no answer—I took him to the India-house and no one was there—he said the tarpaulin was with the wheat-in leaving the India-house, I asked him what he thought of his friend—he said he was led into the trap, and was too old to be caught.
Cross-examined. Q. Was not the wharfinger examined? A. The stable-keeper was who had taken charge of the wharf—the wharfinger was there-his name is Norton—he was asked whether Stemp was in the habit of having consignments to him on that wharf, and he said he was.
MR. BALLANTINE. Q. Did the prisoner say any thing to Stemp, with reference to the property being consigned to him? A. Yes; when he said he brought it for Mr. Stemp, Mr. Stemp was sitting there, and I said, "Is that Mr. Stemp?"—he said, "Yes," he turned round and said, "How do you know me, I gave you no orders?"
JOHN DUNGATE . I reside in South Lambeth. I was employed at the wharf, to get some corn out of the barge.
NOT GUILTY .
[trialAccount t18380820-2078]
2078. ARTHUR BIGGS was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of August 1 handkerchief, value 2 s., the goods of Mary Parker.-2nd COUNT, stating it to be the goods of Richard Sargent.
MARY PARKER . I am a widow, and keep a chandler's-shop at Batter—on the 9th of August, five or six young gentlemen came to my house—I had some silk handkerchiefs on my premises—I missed one of them when they were gone—this is the one—( looking at it)-Richard Serjeant is a grocer in Wands worth—I had it to wash for him.
Prisoner. A boy asked me to go over the bridge, and I did—he met our boys, and I went with them to the door. Witness. I cannot say the prisoner was in the shop.
THOMAS DALEY ( police-constable V 127.) Prisoner. When the boys saw the policeman coming, one of them aid, Take care of this handkerchief"—I did so, and the officer took me.

[trialAccount t18380820-2079]
2079. JAMES LITTLE was indicted for embezzlement.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the Prosecution.
EDWARD CHRISTIANSON . I am a grocer, and live in Bermondsey-street, out wark. The prisoner was in my employ for about five weeks-in consequence of something, I marked two half-crowns and one shilling, and gave it Phoebe Ann Davis on Wednesday evening, August the 8th—I are her some directions—I afterwards saw her in the shop—I was looking rough a crevice on the stairs—the prisoner was there—I saw Davis purchase some goods and tender some money—she left the shop—there were other customers there—I still remained there—I immediately went to the till and took the silver out—I found the two marked half-crowns and two sixpences, which were not marked—the marked shilling was gone—I had been the till before the marked money was paid by Davis—there were no sixpences in it then, nor any money whatever, except a few halfpence—I sent for a policeman, had the prisoner searched, and the marked shilling was found on him—I challenged him with the theft—he told me he had stolen is that morning, and the money was in his waistcoat pocket—the officer was present—I will swear he made use of that expression.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. What was it he said? A. "I have taken 2 s., it is in my pocket, what I have taken"—I am quite sure those were the words he used—I saw him serve two customers besides Mrs. Davis, to the amount of 2 s.
PHOEBE ANN DAVIS . Mr. Christianson gave the marked money to me—I purchased goods of the prisoner to the amount of 5 s. 7 d.—I paid two half-crowns and one shilling, all three marked—I had five penny-pieces in change—he put the money into the till, and I left the shop.
JAMES WHOWALL ( police-constable M 166.) I was called in to take the prisoner. I examined him, and found one marked shilling, which I took out of his waistcoat-pocket—I found 13 s. 3 1/4 d. altogether on him, among which Mr. Christianson recognised one shilling—I was present when he charged the prisoner with robbing him—he first said, "You scoundrel, you have robbed me"—the prisoner said he had taken 2 s.—I
searched him, and found the marked shilling on him—the conversation took place before the search-no further conversation took place—I took him away.
Cross-examined. Q. I believe you forgot to say any thing of this before the Magistrate? A. No, Sir, I did not—I mentioned it at the Town Hall—my deposition was read before I signed it—I did not ask to add any thing to it.
COURT to EDWARD CHRISTIANSON. Q. Do you mean that no other customers bought more than 2 s. worth? A. I will swear to that no other was watching all the while.
NOT GUILTY .

[trialAccount t18380820-2083]
2083. JOHN RYAN, DANIEL DALEY , and JOHN JUDEN , were indicted for stealing on the 19th of July, 4 gowns, value 3 l.; 10 yards of merino value 10 s.; I shawl, value 4 s.; and 1 petticoat, value 1 s.; the goods of Jane Rands; and CAROLINE JUDEN was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing them to have been stolen; against the statute, &c.—3rd COUNT, for feloniously receiving the same of a certain evil-disposed person.
JANE RANDS . I live in Grafton-place, Kentish-town, with Mr. Keghill, and am single—this is my property—I missed it on the 19th of July, between one and two o'clock in the day, from the middle parlour of the house—I had left the window open—it looks into a mews—I saw them all safe at one o'clock—I saw three persons sitting down when I was there, I could not identify them—I saw my property at Union Hall—I know this to be mine.
RICHARD DEYKIN . I am a constable of Kentish-town. Between eleven and said twelve o'clock on the 19th of July, these three lads came into the town—I followed them a little way, but a gentleman stopped me, which caused me to lose them—they came in by the Castle, at Kentish-town—they were all in company together.
EDMUND NORTH . I am an assistant to a pawnbroker, and live at No. Stone's-end, Borough. I produce one gown, which was pawned by the female prisoner on the 19th of July, I think in the afternoon, I am not quit certain, in the name of Ann Jones.
WILLIAM REEVE . I am a pawnbroker, in Blackman-street, Borough. I produce one silk gown pawned by the female prisoner in the name of Mary Butler.
JOHN CRESWELL . I am a pawnbroker, in Blackman-street, Borough. I produce a gown and shawl pawned by the female prisoner on the 19th and 21st of July—one in the name of Ann Juden, and one in the name of Ann Jones-when she pledged the gown she said it did not belong to her.
JAMES BROOKS ( police-constable L 118.) On the 29th of July, I apprehended the three boys for a robbery at Vauxhall, and from further information I took the female prisoner into custody on the 31st—I told her to take off the petticoat which she was wearing at the station-house, which she did—I asked her who sent her to pledge the four gowns—he said her brother and others—I found a duplicate of a shawl and this piece of merino at her two other's mother's lodgings—she said she got them from her brother—he was not present.
CHARLES BURGESS GOFF ( police-constable L 63.) The female prisoner had this bonnet on at Union Hall—she said her brother gave her the merino to make the bonnet, for pawning the four gowns.
John Juden's Defence. I went home and saw my sister with a young lad—she said he had brought the things there for her to pledge.
Caroline Juden's Defence. The young man brought the things and
asked me to pawn them—he said they were his sister's, and she would give any one the petticoat and the merino to pawn the other things—I had no idea they were stolen.
(Caroline Juden received a goon character.)
CAROLINE JUDEN— GUILTY . Aged 14.— Judgment Respited.

DANIEL DALEY
JOHN JUDEN
NOT GUILTY
[trialAccount t18380820-2084]
2084. JOHN RYAN, DANIEL DALEY , and JOHN JUDEN , were again indicted for stealing, on the 13th of July, 2 shirts, value 3 s.; 1 gown, value 3 s.; 4 pairs of stockings, value 2 s.; 1 table-cloth, value 2 s.; 5 towels, value 2 s.; 5 cravats, value 1 s.; 1 handkerchief, value 1 s.; 1 shift, value 1 s.; 1 bed gown, value 1 s.; 1 pair of trowsers, value 1 s.; 1 night-cap, value 6 d.; and 1 basket, value 1 s.; the goods of Joseph Parsons.
SARAH PARSONS . I am the wife of Joseph Parsons, of Salamanca-wharf, Lambeth. On Friday the 13th of July, I lost a basket of lines out of the front-parlour-part was mangled, and part was folded ready to iron—my house is in a public street—I saw it safe at half-past two o'clock and at three o'clock I received information, and missed it—I did not see any of the prisoners near my house-here are two aprons and part of as apron, a night-cap, one towel, and five stockings—these are part of what I lost.
ELIZA MARY PICKLE . I live with my mother, Mrs. Pickle, right opposite on site Mrs. Parsons-at three o'clock, on the 13th of July, I saw Jhon Ryan and Daley standing by the window, and Juden was at the corner near there—I did not see them do any thing.
JAMES WHITE . On the 13th of July, I was on the top of my house which is opposite to Mrs. Parsons'—I saw Ryan and Daley near the prosecutor's window for about ten minutes—I then saw Ryan get in at the window, and hand out a basket of linen to Daley-before I could get down to give information, they were gone—I did not see Juden at the time this was taken—he was sitting down opposite, but I could not see him.
CHARLES BURGESS GOFF ( police-constable L 31.) On Sunday the 29th of July, the three prisoners were in custody at our station-house—I took Daley's stockings off, and they were identified by Mrs. Parsons—I then went to Juden's mother, and found a clothes-basket and two stockings, and these other things, which were identified by Mrs. Parsons.
JAMES BROOK ( police-constable L 118.) I apprehended the prisoners on the 29th—I found this one stocking on Ryan.
Ryan's Defence. I had a pair of stockings on, which they swore to—they took one stocking off, and not the other—I had bought them at a clothes shop.
Daley's Defence. I bought two odd stockings at the same place, and a shirt, and they took my stockings off.
RYAN *— GUILTY . Aged 12.
DALEY *— GUILTY . Aged 17.
JUDEN *— GUILTY . Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
Before Mr. Common Sergeant.

2085. JOHN HAYNES was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of July, 1 pair of trowsers, value 1 s.; 1 pair of drawers, value 1 s.; 2 pairs of stockings, value 1 s.; 1 waistcoat, value 6 d.; 7 shirts, value 6 s.; 2 combs, value 2 d.; 1 cap, value 1 s. 6 d.; 3 jackets, value 14 s.; and 1 s., the goods and monies of William Fitzpatrick, in a certain vessel on the navigable river Thames.
The prosecutor being called, did not appear.
NOT GUILTY .
[trialAccount t18380820-2086]
2086. SARAH BLENCOWE was indicted for bigamy.
JAMES GREEN . I am clerk of St. John the Evangelist District Church, Waterloo-road, Lambeth. I know the prisoner—I remember her being married there on the 8th of October, 1835—I was a subscribing witness—I have no doubt she is the person that was married.
MARY ANN WILLIS . The prisoner is my daughter. I was present when she was married by banns, on the 8th of October, 1835, to Nathaniel Blencowe—he is now living—I saw him yesterday in Court.
SOPHIA HEAP . I have a son named William Heap. I was present on Palm Sunday, the 8th of April last, when he was married at St. Mary, Lambeth, to the prisoner—she gave her name as Sarah Willett—my son had no money—he is here.
Prisoner. My first husband ill-used me, and turned me out of doors.
GUILTY . Aged.— Recommended to mercy by the Jury.— Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18380820-2087]
2087. JAMES FOSTER was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 2 spoons, value 4 s., the goods of Robert Barratt, his master; to which he pleaded
GUILTY . Aged.— Confined Eight Days, and Whipped.
Before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18380820-2088]
2088. JOHN HAZELL was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 5 s., the goods of Edward Bidwell, from his person.
EDWARD BIDWELL . I live at Camberwell, and am a miller. On the afternoon of Saturday, the 18th of August, about five o'clock, I was in the fair at Camberwell—I felt a tug at my pocket—I turned round immediately, and saw the prisoner drop my handkerchief—he was close to me—I gave him into custody.
Prisoner. I saw it lying on the ground—the gentleman took hold of me. Witness. I saw it drop from his hand, and picked it up myself.
RICHARD GOLDINO ( police-constable V 117.) I saw the prisoner at the fair in the morning, and in the afternoon, about five o'clock, I saw him with the prosecutor's handkerchief in his hand—he dropped it as the prosecutor states.
( Property produced and sworn to)
Prisoner. I did not start away from home before three o'clock—I was looking at one of the shows, and saw the handkerchief on the ground.
GUILTY . Aged 12.— Confined Three Months.
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
[trialAccount t18380820-2089]
2089. PATRICK LUCY was indicted for an assault on Mary Haley, with intent to violate her.
NOT GUILTY .
ADJOURNED TILL MONDAY, SEPTEMBER 17.
